Experiments show that QUAKE-CD substantiall<sub>y</sub> improves pixel-level VLM change detection. Mask Decoding. The decoding function $\mathcal { D } ~ : ~ \mathcal { L } _ { Q } ~ $ $\{ 0 , 1 \} ^ { H \times W }$ efectively reverses E by parsing the QUAKE <sub>sequence, reso</sub>l<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng run-</sub>l<sub>eng</sub>th <sub>compress</sub>i<sub>on, an</sub>d <sub>recurs</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y</sub> rendering quadtree nodes back into 32×32 pixel blocks. Consequently, D operates as a purely non-parametric mapping, <sub>c</sub>i<sub>rcumven</sub>ti<sub>ng neura</sub>l <sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>ensur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h <sub>e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>ency</sub> d<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th RL <sub>rewar</sub>d <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Additionally, a pad/crop discount penalizes topologi-<sub>ca</sub>l di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on m</sub>i<sub>sma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es necess</sub>it<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>a</sub>llb<sub>ac</sub>k <sub>a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> while an empty-GT guard outright neutralizes the trivial ex-<sub>p</sub>l<sub>o</sub>it<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>egy</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>perpe</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>pure</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>groun</sub>d (see details in Appendix C).

GRPO Optimization. We optimize the VLM policy starting from the cold-start checkpoint utilizing the GRPO objective (Guo et al. 2025), usin<sub>g</sub> the reward:

$$
R ( \mathbf { y } , M ) = ( 1 - \mu ) R _ { \mathrm { q } } ( \mathbf { y } , M ) + \mu R _ { \mathrm { t } } ( I _ { 1 } , I _ { 2 } , \mathbf { y } )\tag{3}
$$

## 4 Experiments

## Experimental Setup

Setup. All experiments utilize the QUAKE-CoT established i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 3<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e a</sub>ll<sub>oca</sub>t<sub>e</sub> 80% <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng se</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo-</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>age</sub> <sub>curr</sub>i<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>um</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>reserve</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> 20% <sub>exc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or rewar</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d RL<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ramewor</sub>k i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>a</sub>l<sub>-</sub> ized from Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct (Bai et al. 2025). Trainin<sub>g</sub> is executed via ms-swift (Zhao et al. 2024) on ei<sub>g</sub>ht NVIDIA H200 GPUs, while inference is served throu<sub>g</sub>h vLLM (Kwon et al. 2023). Both SFT sta<sub>g</sub>es and the reward model em<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub> LoRA (Hu et al. 2022) with rank 64, with the reward model i<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>a</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>a</sub> f<sub>res</sub>h b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>one</sub> <sub>copy.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> RL <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ase</sub> <sub>op-</sub> timizes the <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> via GRPO (Guo et al. 2025) usin<sub>g</sub> ei<sub>g</sub>ht rollouts per prompt, and a KL coeficient of 0.04. Appendix D <sub>ca</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ogs</sub> th<sub>e compre</sub>h<sub>ens</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> h<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>er spec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>

Baselines. We compare against three complementary baseli<sub>ne groups.</sub> Fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>, we</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve spec</sub>i<sub>a</sub>li<sub>s</sub>t <sub>remo</sub>t<sub>e</sub> sensin<sub>g</sub> chan<sub>g</sub>e detectors: BIT (Chen, Qi, and Shi 2021), Chan<sub>g</sub>eFormer (Bandara and Patel 2022), and Chan<sub>g</sub>e-CLIP (Don<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024). Second, we evaluate <sub>p</sub>ixel-level VLMs across two <sub>p</sub>revailin<sub>g p</sub>aradi<sub>g</sub>ms. LISA (Lai et al. 2024), GLaMM (Rasheed et al. 2024), and PixelLM (Ren et al. 2024) <sub>p</sub>erform decoder-based mask <sub>p</sub>rediction, while Text4Se<sub>g</sub> (Lan et al. 2025) and RSUniVLM (Liu and Lian 2026) serialize masks autore<sub>g</sub>ressivel<sub>y</sub>. S<sub>p</sub>ecialist chan<sub>g</sub>e detectors and RSUniVLM are retrained on the QUAKE-C<sub>o</sub>T tr<sub>a</sub>inin<sub>g sp</sub>lit<sub>.</sub> RSUniVLM n<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y accep</sub>t<sub>s</sub> RS bi<sub>-</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>rs, w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l VLM<sub>s as-</sub> sume sin<sub>g</sub>le-ima<sub>g</sub>e in<sub>p</sub>uts. Followin<sub>g</sub> (N<sub>g</sub>u<sub>y</sub>en et al. 2025; Xu, Zhu, and Yan<sub>g</sub> 2025), we concatenate the bi-tem<sub>p</sub>oral <sub>p</sub>a<sup>i</sup>r $( I _ { 1 } , I _ { 2 } )$ <sub>a</sub>l<sub>ong</sub> th<sub>e w</sub>idth di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on an</sub>d fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>une</sub> th<sub>em on</sub> the QUAKE-CoT trainin<sub>g</sub> split under their ori<sub>g</sub>inal trainin<sub>g</sub> <sub>rec</sub>i<sub>pes.</sub> Thi<sub>r</sub>d<sub>,</sub> f<sub>or reason</sub>i<sub>ng-qua</sub>lit<sub>y eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on, we</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub> Teochat (Irvin et al. 2025) and EarthDial (Soni et al. 2025). Th<sub>ese</sub> t<sub>e</sub>m<sub>po</sub>r<sub>a</sub>l RS VLM<sub>s ge</sub>n<sub>e</sub>r<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>n<sub>ge</sub> d<sub>esc</sub>ri<sub>p-</sub> tions but do not predict dense masks. Additional experimental results on large images, out-of-domain dataset, qualitative interpretability, and parameter sensitivity are reported in Appendix E.

## Dense Change Mask Generation

For each prediction, we decode the extracted QUAKE span into a binar<sub>y</sub> mask with D and re<sub>p</sub>ort Precision, Recall, and F1 <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er accumu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>cs an</sub>d <sub>per-</sub>i<sub>mage means.</sub> As shown in Table 1, QUAKE-CD attains specialist-level <sub>mas</sub>k <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y w</sub>hil<sub>e re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng a un</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oregress</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er-</sub> f<sub>ace</sub> f<sub>or</sub> d<sub>ense</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> It<sub>s</sub> <sub>accu-</sub> mulated F1 of 78.31% surpasses BIT by 1.37% and remains within 3.13% of the strongest specialist model, Change-Former. Under per-ima<sub>g</sub>e evaluation, QUAKE-CD reaches 73.18% F1 and outperforms all three specialist models. It f<sub>ur</sub>th<sub>er ac</sub>hi<sub>eves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>es</sub>t <sub>reca</sub>ll <sub>among a</sub>ll <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s un-</sub> d<sub>er</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>s.</sub> A<sub>ga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l VLM<sub>s,</sub> QUAKE-CD surpasses PixelLM by 51.04% in accumulated F1 and LISA-7B by 51.27% in per-image F1. Existing VLM b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes ex</sub>hibit <sub>a pronounce</sub>d <sub>reca</sub>ll<sub>–prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> i<sub>m</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ance:</sub> th<sub>ey recover</sub> b<sub>roa</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ange reg</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce ex</sub>t<sub>ens</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se pos</sub>iti<sub>ves.</sub> E<sub>ven</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ronges</sub>t VLM b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne reac</sub>h<sub>es on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> 50.12% accumulated precision and 48.76% per-image precision. Text4Seg exemplifies this regime, attaining 84.07% accumulated recall but only 14.52% precision and thus 24.76% F1<sub>:</sub> it<sub>s</sub> fl<sub>a</sub>t <sub>pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>-gr</sub>id <sub>ser</sub>i<sub>a</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on ass</sub>i<sub>gns un</sub>if<sub>orm reso</sub>l<sub>u-</sub> ti<sub>on across</sub> th<sub>e scene an</sub>d <sub>canno</sub>t <sub>reso</sub>l<sub>ve</sub> th<sub>e sma</sub>ll<sub>,</sub> f<sub>rag-</sub> mented objects characteristic of change detection. In contrast, QUAKE-CD raises precision to 70.99% and 68.77% while preserving strong recall at 87.31% and 84.09%. This <sub>a</sub>d<sub>van</sub>t<sub>age s</sub>t<sub>ems</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ve a</sub>ll<sub>oca</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>qua</sub>dt<sub>ree</sub> t<sub>o-</sub> k<sub>ens aroun</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ange</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>es, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>suppresses spur</sub>i<sub>ous</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>eras</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> fi<sub>ne-gra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ures.</sub> RSU<sub>n</sub>iVLM <sub>a</sub>d<sub>op</sub>t<sub>s</sub> fl<sub>a</sub>t t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>-as-mas</sub>k f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>hil<sub>e accep</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bi<sub>-</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y.</sub> It<sub>s</sub> <sub>pers</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>reca</sub>ll<sub>–prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> i<sub>m</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>-</sub> ance isolates the QUAKE-CD <sub>g</sub>ain to hierarchical quadtree <sub>enco</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> bi<sub>-</sub>t<sub>empora</sub>l i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

QUAKE-CD also achieves a favorable balance between accuracy and eficiency. Its throughput reaches 74.27 pairs/s, at least 1.85× that of decoder-based VLMs, as native text <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>on avo</sub>id<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e over</sub>h<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a segmen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>er.</sub> It<sub>s</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub> t<sub>o</sub> fl<sub>a</sub>t <sub>ser</sub>i<sub>a</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s,</sub> i<sub>n-</sub> cluding Text4Seg at 86.54 pairs/s and RSUniVLM at 82.30 <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>rs</sub>/<sub>s,</sub> d<sub>esp</sub>it<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>sequen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d by the hierarchical QUAKE syntax. In return, QUAKE-CD im<sub>p</sub>r<sub>oves accu</sub>m<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d F1 <sub>ove</sub>r T<sub>ex</sub>t4S<sub>eg a</sub>nd RSUniVLM by 53.55% and 15.97%, respectively, establishing a stronger t<sub>ra</sub>d<sub>e-o</sub>f b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l fid<sub>e</sub>lit<sub>y.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e-</sub> t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>roug</sub>h<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>se</sub>t<sub>up</sub> i<sub>s prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> D<sub>.</sub>

## Reasoning Quality Evaluation

Si<sub>nce mas</sub>k <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs canno</sub>t <sub>ver</sub>if<sub>y w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ms</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>em</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> bi<sub>-</sub>t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence, we eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <think> and <answer> trajectories. Although we report BLEU<sub>-</sub>1<sub>,</sub> ROUGE<sub>-</sub>1<sub>, a</sub>nd METEOR f<sub>o</sub>r l<sub>ex</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>ove</sub>rl<sub>ap,</sub> T<sub>a-</sub> bl<sub>e</sub> 2 <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>gener</sub>i<sub>c</sub> VLM<sub>s score a</sub>b<sub>norma</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> l<sub>ow un-</sub> der n-gram metrics, which penalize paraphrasing and verb<sub>ose reason</sub>i<sub>ng ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an seman</sub>ti<sub>c correc</sub>t<sub>ness.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere-</sub> f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t th<sub>em</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>cs-aware</sub> <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> evaluators and a human-rating protocol. Reranker Score (Qwen3-Reranker-4B) assesses semantic fidelit<sub>y</sub>, com<sub>p</sub>leteness, and structural coherence. GPT-Judge Accuracy (GPT-<sup>-./FIM12P4IRP4/MS</sup> 5.5-2026-04-23) quantifies semantic consistenc<sub>y</sub> a<sub>g</sub>ainst human-verified references (<sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t tem<sub>p</sub>lates in Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 15'\* and 16). Human Score averages acceptance rates from two independent annotators over 1,000 randomly sampled out-<sub>I</sub>><sup>A</sup> <sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>assesse</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>oca</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>cor-</sub><sup>(\*</sup><sub>1</sub><sup>c</sup> rectness, reaching 87.5% inter-annotator agreement.

T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 2 di<sub>sen</sub>t<sub>ang</sub>l<sub>es sca</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>rom groun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d bi<sub>-</sub>t<sub>empora</sub>l supervision. Under traditional metrics, QUAKE-CD outperforms all baselines, achieving 49.30 BLEU-1 and 40.24<sup>!'</sup> METEOR. On semantics-aware metrics, scalin<sub>g</sub> Qwen3-VL&\* from 8B to 235B raises the reranker score from 27.76 to 56.20 and GPT-judge accuracy from 47.78% to 64.53%, as greater <sub>capac</sub>it<sub>y</sub> i<sub>mproves</sub> fl<sub>uency</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>coarse</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>aus</sub>ibilit<sub>y.</sub> Yet scale alone does not close the <sub>g</sub>roundin<sub>g</sub> <sub>g</sub>ap: QUAKE-CD exceeds the same-8B baseline by 31.25% in human score and still leads the 235B model by 13.20%. RSUniVLM also trails QUAKE-CD by 18.85% in human score, indicating th<sub>a</sub>t it<sub>s</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ange-cap</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>nsu</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t f<sub>or</sub> <sub>cap-</sub> t<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> k<sub>ey</sub> bi<sub>-</sub>t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub>l<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ange.</sub> Th<sub>e agreemen</sub>t <sub>across</sub> l<sub>ex</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l<sub>, seman</sub>ti<sub>c, an</sub>d h<sub>uman eva</sub>l<sub>ua-</sub> t<sub>ors</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e ga</sub>i<sub>n</sub> i<sub>s ro</sub>b<sub>us</sub>t <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an an ar</sub>tif<sub>ac</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f any single judge.

## Ablation Studies

Minimum Leaf Size. Figure 3 varies the minimum leaf size ℓ, which controls the trade-of between spatial resolution and sequence complexit<sub>y</sub>, under fixed SFT settings. Reducing ℓ from 16 to 2 increases the average sequence length from 57 to 523 tokens, yet mask quality does not improve monotonically. F1 reaches its maximum at ℓ=8 and drops sharply at ℓ=2. This degradation reflects a learnability bottleneck rather th<sub>an a represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l li<sub>m</sub>it<sub>: a</sub>lth<sub>oug</sub>h fi<sub>ner</sub> l<sub>eaves can en-</sub> <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>arper</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>es,</sub> l<sub>ong</sub> <sub>recurs</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> <sub>sequences</sub> i<sub>ncrease</sub> <sub>grammar</sub> <sub>v</sub>i<sub>o</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>an</sub>d di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>m</sub>i<sub>sma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es,</sub> <sub>wea</sub>k<sub>en</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th SFT <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d RL <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> At th<sub>e</sub> <sub>oppos</sub>it<sub>e</sub> <sub>ex-</sub> treme, ℓ=16 reduces the token budget but quantizes each 16×16 patch into a single bit, which erodes boundary precision. The acceptance rate of annotator a for model m i<sub>s</sub>

$$
A _ { m } ^ { ( a ) } = \frac { 1 } { N } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N } h _ { m , i } ^ { ( a ) } ,\tag{8}
$$

and the reported Human Score for model m averages the two annotators<sup>’</sup> acce<sub>p</sub>tance rates:

$$
H _ { m } = \frac { 1 } { 2 } \left( A _ { m } ^ { ( 1 ) } + A _ { m } ^ { ( 2 ) } \right) = \frac { 1 } { 2 N } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N } \sum _ { a = 1 } ^ { 2 } { h _ { m , i } ^ { ( a ) } } .\tag{9}
$$

I<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er-anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>or</sub> <sub>agreemen</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> <sub>exac</sub>t l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>agree-</sub> ment over all MN evaluated outputs:

$$
A _ { \mathrm { i n t e r } } = \frac { 1 } { M N } \sum _ { m = 1 } ^ { M } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N } \mathcal { k } \Big [ h _ { m , i } ^ { ( 1 ) } = h _ { m , i } ^ { ( 2 ) } \Big ] = 8 7 . 5 \% .\tag{10}
$$

## C Reward Construction Details

## Rollout Generation and IoU Scoring

For each prompt x in the 20% RL subset of the QUAKE-C<sub>o</sub>T d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>, we</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub> $K = 1 0$ <sup>res</sup>p<sup>onses</sup> $\{ y _ { 1 } , \dots , y _ { K } \} \sim \pi _ { \theta _ { \mathrm { S F T } } } ( \cdot \mid \mathbf { x } )$ f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e curr</sub>i<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>um-</sub>SFT <sub>po</sub>l<sub>-</sub> icy. Each response y<sub>i</sub> is parsed to extract its quadtree mask <sup>se</sup>qu<sup>ence</sup> $y _ { \mathrm { s e g } } ^ { ( i ) }$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sequence</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>sequen</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> <sub>processe</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> deterministic decoder D to <sub>y</sub>ield the binar<sub>y</sub> mask <sub>p</sub>rediction $\hat { M } ^ { ( i ) } = \mathcal { D } ( y _ { \mathrm { s e g } } ^ { ( i ) } )$

W<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y o</sub>f <sub>every ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t b<sub>y com-</sub> <sub>p</sub>utin<sub>g</sub> its Intersection over Union (IoU) with the <sub>g</sub>round-truth mask M:

$$
\mathrm { I o U } ( y _ { i } ) = { \frac { \lvert { \hat { M } } ^ { ( i ) } \cap M \rvert } { \lvert { \hat { M } } ^ { ( i ) } \cup M \rvert } }\tag{12}
$$

F<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> $K = 1 0$ <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t th<sub>ree</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>es</sub> <sub>spann</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> I<sub>o</sub>U <sub>spec</sub>tr<sub>u</sub>m<sub>:</sub>

$$

$$

$$

H<sub>ere</sub> $y _ { a }$ <sub>an</sub>d $y _ { b }$ <sub>are</sub> th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>es</sub>t<sub>-</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>owes</sub>t<sub>-</sub>I<sub>o</sub>U <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> $y _ { c }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erme</sub>di<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ose</sub> I<sub>o</sub>U <sub>s</sub>it<sub>s</sub> f<sub>ar</sub>th<sub>es</sub>t f<sub>rom</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>en</sub>d<sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>measure</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a</sub>b<sub>so</sub>l<sub>u</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sup>I</sup>oU <sub>g</sub>aps.

A <sub>pre</sub>f<sub>erence compar</sub>i<sub>son</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween any</sub> t<sub>wo can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> $( y _ { i } , y _ { j } )$ i<sub>s</sub> <sub>cons</sub>id<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>va</sub>lid <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> if th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> di<sub>screp-</sub> <sub>ancy</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gn</sub>ifi<sub>can</sub>t <sub>enoug</sub>h t<sub>o</sub> <sub>warran</sub>t <sub>a</sub> di<sub>scern</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> dif<sub>erence.</sub> W<sub>e en</sub>f<sub>orce</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>a marg</sub>i<sub>n c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld $\tau = 0 . 1 $

$$

$$

B<sub>y</sub> in<sub>co</sub>r<sub>po</sub>r<sub>a</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> th<sub>e g</sub>r<sub>ou</sub>nd<sub>-</sub>tr<sub>u</sub>th <sub>seque</sub>n<sub>ce</sub> $y _ { \mathrm { g t } }$ (considered the absolute u<sub>pp</sub>er bound) and the fabricated hard ne<sub>g</sub>ati<sub>ve</sub> $y _ { \mathrm { n e g } }$ (the absolute lower bound), we establish a strin<sub>g</sub>ent, <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>-</sub>ti<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> hi<sub>erarc</sub>h<sub>y:</sub>

F<sub>rom</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> hi<sub>erarc</sub>h<sub>y, we ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t th<sub>e pre</sub>f<sub>erence se</sub>t

$$
\mathcal D _ { \mathrm { p r e f } } = \{ ( y _ { c } , y _ { r } ) \ | \ y _ { c } \succ y _ { r } \} ,\tag{18}
$$

i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>an</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>rs</sub> <sub>suc</sub>h <sub>as</sub> $( y _ { \mathrm { g t } } , y _ { \mathrm { n e g } } ) , ( y _ { b } , y _ { \mathrm { n e g } } ) , ( y _ { a } , y _ { c } ) ,$ <sub>an</sub>d $( y _ { c } , y _ { b } )$ , provided the τ gap condition is satisfied for <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>sons.</sub> Th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>rs</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>aggrega</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct reward model. The neural reward model is optimized using the following objective:

The Quadtree Re-<sub>war</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> f<sub>orma</sub>t <sub>comp</sub>li<sub>ance</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>spa-</sub> ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>accuracy:</sub>

$$

If th<sub>e</sub> <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s</sub> $\mathcal { L } _ { Q }$ <sub>p</sub>arsin<sub>g</sub>—missin<sub>g</sub> <seg> wra<sub>p</sub>- <sub>per, unrecogn</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens, a</sub> f<sub>or</sub>bidd<sub>en</sub> $< \mathsf { Q } 0 0 0 0 0 >$ <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e,</sub> <sub>an</sub> RLE count below 2, or a child count not matching $\textstyle \sum _ { i } b _ { i }$ for any <Qabcd> node—grammar gating forces $R _ { q } = \overline { { 0 . 0 } }$ Oth<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se:</sub>

• Structure Score $( R _ { \mathrm { s t r u c t } } ) { : }$ Th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>gr</sub>id i<sub>s compare</sub>d <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t th<sub>e expec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> $( H _ { e } , \bar { W _ { e } } )$ <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a row-w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>error ra</sub>t<sub>es an</sub>d <sub>an exac</sub>t<sub>-a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t b<sub>onus:</sub>

$$
\epsilon _ { h } = \operatorname* { m i n } \bigr ( 1 , | H _ { p } - H _ { e } | / H _ { e } \bigr ) ,\tag{22}
$$

$$
{ \epsilon _ { w } } = \frac { 1 } { { { H _ { e } } } } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { { H _ { e } } } { \operatorname* { m i n } } { \left( 1 , \ : | { W _ { i } } - { W _ { e } } | / { W _ { e } } \right) } ,\tag{23}

$$

$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $H _ { p }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>row coun</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d $W _ { i }$ th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>og</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l width of row i after RLE expansion.

• Content Score $( R _ { \mathrm { c o n t e n t } } ) { : }$ Th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d bi<sub>nary mas</sub>k Mˆ is scored against the ground truth M via the Tversky ind<sub>ex</sub> $\mathcal { T } _ { \alpha , \beta }$ <sub>w</sub>ith $( \alpha , \beta ) \^ = \ ( 0 . 3 , 0 . 7 )$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se</sub> <sub>nega</sub>ti<sub>ves more s</sub>t<sub>rong</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>an</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se pos</sub>iti<sub>ves.</sub> W<sub>r</sub>iti<sub>ng</sub> $\mathrm { T P } = | M \cap \hat { M } | , \mathrm { F P } = | \hat { M } \setminus M |$ <sub>, an</sub>d $\mathrm { F N } = | M \setminus \hat { M } |$

$$
\mathcal { T } _ { \alpha , \beta } ( M , \hat { M } ) = \frac { \mathrm { T P } } { \mathrm { T P } + \alpha \mathrm { F P } + \beta \mathrm { F N } } .\tag{25}
$$

Th<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>score</sub> i<sub>s non-</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub> t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>mas</sub>k <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> f<sub>oregroun</sub>d<sub>: w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> $| M | = 0 .$ , an exactempty prediction earns a residual reward of 0.2 to pre-<sub>ven</sub>t <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>apse</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>groun</sub>d <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> <sub>any</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se-</sub> positive prediction yields 0. For active masks $\left( \left| M \right| > 0 \right) :$

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { R _ { \mathrm { c o n t e n t } } = 0 . 7 0 \mathcal { T } _ { \alpha , \beta } ( M , \hat { M } ) s _ { \mathrm { a l i g n } } } \\ & { \qquad + 0 . 0 3 \mathcal { k } [ \mathcal { T } \geq 0 . 4 5 \wedge \mathrm { s t r i c t } ] , } \end{array}\tag{26}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $s _ { \mathrm { a l i g n } } = 1 . 0$ <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>t d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>e an</sub>d $s _ { \mathrm { a l i g n } } = 0 . 8 0$ <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e pa</sub>d/<sub>crop</sub> f<sub>a</sub>llb<sub>ac</sub>k <sub>was</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ggere</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e a</sub>dditi<sub>ve</sub> +0.03 rewards strict-decoded predictions whose Tversky <sub>score c</sub>l<sub>ears</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary-preserva</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>.</sub>

ThinkAnswer Reward Formulation (R<sub>t</sub>). Th<sub>e rewar</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l $R _ { \phi }$ t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre-</sub> <sub>ce</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> RM <sup>sta</sup>g<sup>e</sup> scores th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d $\scriptstyle < \mathrm { t h i n k } > \dotsc < < / \mathrm { t h i n k } > < \dotsc \dotsc < / \mathrm { a n s w e r } > \dotsc \dotsc < / \mathrm { a n s w e r } >$ <sub>segmen</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>.</sub> T<sub>o s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bili<sub>se</sub> th<sub>e rewar</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e across</sub> <sub>quer</sub>i<sub>es, we app</sub>l<sub>y query-w</sub>i<sub>se cen</sub>t<sub>re</sub>d <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gmo</sub>id <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>sa</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sup>o</sup>v<sup>er</sup> <sup>the</sup> g<sup>ro</sup>up $\mathcal { S } _ { \mathbf { x } }$ <sub>o</sub>f $\dot { K } = 8$ rollouts sharing prompt x:

$$
R _ { t } ( y _ { i } ) = \sigma \big ( R _ { \phi } ( y _ { i } \mid \mathbf { x } ) - \bar { R } _ { \phi } ( \mathbf { x } ) \big ) ,\tag{27}
$$

$$
\bar { R } _ { \phi } ( \mathbf { x } ) = \frac { 1 } { | S _ { \mathbf { x } } | } \sum _ { y _ { j } \in S _ { \mathbf { x } } } R _ { \phi } ( y _ { j } \mid \mathbf { x } ) .\tag{28}

## Scalability to High-Resolution Imagery

Although our model is trained with 256 × 256 image pairs, <sub>prac</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>remo</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>sens</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>magery</sub> <sub>o</sub>ft<sub>en</sub> <sub>covers</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> l<sub>arger</sub> <sub>spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> on the original 1024 × 1024 images from LEVIR-CD and LEVIR<sub>-</sub>CD+<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>-reso</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>arc</sub>hi<sub>-</sub> t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>difi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>co-reg</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ere</sub>d bi<sub>-</sub>t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> i<sub>s</sub> partitioned into a 4 × 4 grid of non-overlapping and spatially aligned 256 × 256 tile pairs. The model processes each pair i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y a</sub>t it<sub>s na</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng reso</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e resu</sub>lt<sub>-</sub> ing QUAKE predictions are decoded into binary tile masks, <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ore</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es, an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>tit<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>-</sub> resolution 1024 × 1024 prediction for evaluation against the <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>groun</sub>d t<sub>ru</sub>th<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l <sub>preserves</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l i<sub>mage</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il <sub>an</sub>d <sub>avo</sub>id<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>oss cause</sub>d b<sub>y res</sub>i<sub>z</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>en</sub>ti<sub>re</sub> i<sub>mage</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng reso</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> N<sub>o over</sub>l<sub>app</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>er-</sub> <sub>ence,</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>-</sub>ti<sub>me a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on, cross-</sub>til<sub>e re</sub>fi<sub>nemen</sub>t<sub>, or</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d f<sub>us</sub>i<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s emp</sub>l<sub>oye</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

## Qualitative Attention Visualization

T<sub>o assess</sub> th<sub>e spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>groun</sub>di<sub>ng o</sub>f th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>p</sub>rocess, we construct two com<sub>p</sub><sup>l</sup>ementar<sub>y</sub> attent<sup>i</sup>on v<sup>i</sup>ews. The token-conditioned visualization projects the attention associated with each generated QUAKE token onto its designated visual tile. <table><tr><td>Prompt for Reranker</td></tr><tr><td>You are an expert evaluator for remote sensing change detection answers.</td></tr><tr><td>Given a reference answer (Query) and a candidate answer (Document),</td></tr><tr><td>judge whether the Document is truly relevant and faithful to the Query.</td></tr><tr><td>Use all of the following criteria jointly: 1) Semantic fidelity: preserve the same change semantics and key entities.</td></tr><tr><td>2) Semantic completeness: do not omit important change details.</td></tr><tr><td>3) Conciseness and length quality: avoid verbose, repetitive, or overly short responses that lose</td></tr><tr><td>meaning. 4) Structural coherence: keep a compatible logical structure</td></tr><tr><td>(main conclusion, key change factors, and causal/contrast relations).</td></tr><tr><td>Note: all four criteria are equally important. No criterion is optional or secondary.</td></tr><tr><td>Semantically correct answers with different wording/order should still be considered relevant.</td></tr><tr><td>Superficially similar wording/structure with wrong conclusions or missing key facts should be</td></tr></table>
